n=3
and
n\geq 5
.
Then we have

\tilde{h}^{\prime}_{3}+\tilde{h}_{4}=h_{1}+h_{2}=h
,
by removing
h^{\prime}_{1}
from both sides of
h_{3}+h_{4}=h^{\prime}_{1}+h_{2}
and adding
h_{1}
to them.
Hence we have
s,\tilde{h}^{\prime}_{3}+\tilde{h}_{4}\models\Sigma
.
Therefore we have (1) since
s,h\models\overrightarrow{\psi}
can be obtained.

Remark 7
.

We note that our unroll collapse properties (Proposition
5
and
6
)
hold for entailments with the points-to predicate, the array predicate,
and the (possibly cyclic) singly-linked and doubly-linked list predicates.
We also note that ours hold for entailments that contain arithmetic.
The original version of unroll collapse is given by Berdine et al.
[
2
]
.
It holds for entailments with only the points-to predicate and the acyclic singly-linked list predicate.
We cannot compare ours and theirs naively, since the singly-linked list predicates in both papers are different for cyclic lists.

7.
Decision Procedure for Arrays and Lists

This section gives our algorithm for checking
the validity of a given entailment, whose antecedent do not contain list predicates.
In the following subsections
7.1
and
7.2
, we implicitly assume that the antecedent of each entailment is list-free.

The procedure first eliminates the list predicates in the succedent of a given entailment.
The resulting entailments only contain the points-to and array predicates, that is, they are entailments in
SLA
.
Then the procedure checks their validity by using the decision procedure of
SLA
.

7.1.
Proof System for Elimination of Lists in Succedents

This subsection gives a proof system for entailments
whose antecedents do not contain list predicates.
Our decision procedure is given as a proof-search procedure
of the proof system.

We define a Presburger formula
\Sigma\to\downarrow t

that means the address
t
is a cell of
\Sigma
.
We also define
{\rm Tm}(\Sigma)
, which is the set of terms in
\Sigma
.
For defining them, we will not implicitly use the commutative law for
*
and the unit law for
Emp
.

{defi}

\Sigma\to\downarrow t
is inductively defined as follows:

\hbox{Emp}\to\downarrow t\overset{{\rm def}}{=}\hbox{False}
,

\hbox{Emp}*\Sigma^{\prime}\to\downarrow t\overset{{\rm def}}{=}\Sigma^{\prime}\to\downarrow t
,

t^{\prime}\mapsto(\hbox{-},\hbox{-})\to\downarrow t\overset{{\rm def}}{=}t=t^{\prime}
,

t^{\prime}\mapsto(\hbox{-},\hbox{-})*\Sigma^{\prime}\to\downarrow t\overset{{\rm def}}{=}t=t^{\prime}\vee\Sigma^{\prime}\to\downarrow t
,

{\rm Arr}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})\to\downarrow t\overset{{\rm def}}{=}t^{\prime}\leq t\leq u^{\prime}
,

{\rm Arr}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\Sigma\to\downarrow t\overset{{\rm def}}{=}t^{\prime}\leq t\leq u^{\prime}\vee\Sigma\to\downarrow t
.

{defi}

{\rm Tm}(\Sigma)
is inductively defined as follows:

{\rm Tm}(\hbox{Emp})\overset{{\rm def}}{=}\emptyset
,

{\rm Tm}(t\mapsto(u_{1},u_{2}))\overset{{\rm def}}{=}\{t,u_{1},u_{2}\}
,

{\rm Tm}({\rm Arr}(t,u))\overset{{\rm def}}{=}\{t,u\}
,

{\rm Tm}(\Sigma_{1}*\Sigma_{2})\overset{{\rm def}}{=}{\rm Tm}(\Sigma_{1})\cup{\rm Tm}(\Sigma_{2})
.

We write
{\rm Tm}(\overrightarrow{\Sigma})
for
\bigcup_{\Sigma\in\overrightarrow{\Sigma}}{\rm Tm}(\Sigma)
.

Lemma 8
.

Suppose
s,h\models\Sigma
. Then

s\models\Sigma\to\downarrow t

if and only if

s(t)\in{\rm Dom}(h)
.

Proof 7.1
.

The claim is shown by induction on
\Sigma
.

Then we define the inference rules which give our algorithm.
The rules are shown in Figure
2
.

\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi}

where
\varphi
is list-free and
\varphi\models\overrightarrow{\psi}
in
SLA

\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi}

if
\varphi
is unsat

\varphi\vdash\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi}

if
\varphi\land\psi
is unsat

t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi\vdash{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}t=t^{\prime}\land t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi\vdash{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}t\neq t^{\prime}\land t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi\vdash{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}

if
t=t^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi}

and
t\neq t^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi}
are satisfiable

t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi\vdash{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi\vdash t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\land\psi,t^{\prime}\mapsto(v,w)*{\rm ls}(v,u^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}

if
\Pi_{\varphi}\models t=t^{\prime}

\varphi\vdash{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}\varphi\vdash t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\land\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}

if
\Pi_{\varphi}\not\models\Sigma_{\varphi}\to\downarrow t^{\prime}

t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi\vdash{\rm dll}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime},v^{\prime},w^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}t=t^{\prime}\land t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi\vdash{\rm dll}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime},v^{\prime},w^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}t\neq t^{\prime}\land t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi\vdash{\rm dll}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime},v^{\prime},w^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}

if
t=t^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi}

and
t\neq t^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi}
are satisfiable

t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi\vdash{\rm dll}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime},v^{\prime},w^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi\vdash t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\land v^{\prime}=w^{\prime}\land\psi,t\mapsto(v,w^{\prime})*{\rm dll}(v,u^{\prime},v^{\prime},t^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}

if
\Pi_{\varphi}\models t=t^{\prime}

\varphi\vdash{\rm dll}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime},v^{\prime},w^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}\varphi\vdash t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\land v^{\prime}=w^{\prime}\land\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}

if
\Pi_{\varphi}\not\models\Sigma_{\varphi}\to\downarrow t^{\prime}

{\rm Arr}(t,v)*\varphi\vdash L(t^{\prime},\overrightarrow{u^{\prime}})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}t\leq t^{\prime}\leq v\land{\rm Arr}(t,v)*\varphi\vdash L(t^{\prime},\overrightarrow{u^{\prime}})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}v<t^{\prime}\land{\rm Arr}(t,v)*\varphi\vdash L(t^{\prime},\overrightarrow{u^{\prime}})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}t^{\prime}<t\land{\rm Arr}(t,v)*\varphi\vdash L(t^{\prime},\overrightarrow{u^{\prime}})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}
,

where
t\leq t^{\prime}\leq v\land\Pi_{\varphi}
and
(t^{\prime}<t\vee v<t^{\prime})\land\Pi_{\varphi}
are satisfiable

L(t^{\prime},\overrightarrow{u^{\prime}})
is
{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})
or
{\rm dll}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime}_{1},u^{\prime}_{2},u^{\prime}_{3})

{\rm Arr}(t,v)*\varphi\vdash{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}{\rm Arr}(t,v)*\varphi\vdash t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\land\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}

if
\Pi_{\varphi}\models t\leq t^{\prime}\leq v

{\rm Arr}(t,v)*\varphi\vdash{\rm dll}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime},v^{\prime},w^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}{\rm Arr}(t,v)*\varphi\vdash t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\land v^{\prime}=w^{\prime}\land\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}

if
\Pi_{\varphi}\models t\leq t^{\prime}\leq v

Figure 2.
Inference rules for the decision procedure

Let
h
and
h^{\prime}
be heaps such that
{\rm Dom}(h)={\rm Dom}(h^{\prime})
.
We write
h\sim_{d}h^{\prime}
if
h(x)=h^{\prime}(x)
holds for any
x\in{\rm Dom}(h)\setminus\{d\}
.

The following lemma is used in the proof of
local completeness of the inference rules.

Lemma 9
.

(1) Suppose that
s,h\models\sigma
,
(a,b)\in{\rm Ran}(h)
and

a\not\in{\rm Dom}(h)\cup\{s(t)\mid t\in{\rm Tm}(\sigma)\}
.
Then
\sigma
is an array atomic formula.

(2) Suppose that
s,h\models\Sigma
,
h(d)=(a,b)
,

a\not\in{\rm Dom}(h)\cup\{s(t)\mid t\in{\rm Tm}(\Sigma)\}

and
h^{\prime}\sim_{d}h
.
Then
s,h^{\prime}\models\Sigma
.

Proof 7.2
.

(1) We show the claim by case analysis of
\sigma
.

The case that
\sigma
is
t\mapsto(u,v)
.
By the assumptions, we have
(a,b)\in{\rm Ran}(h)=\{(s(u),s(v))\}
.
Hence we obtain
a=s(u)\in\{s(t)\mid t\in{\rm Tm}(\sigma)\}
,
which contradicts the assumption.

The case that
\sigma
is
{\rm ls}(t,u)
.
By the assumptions, we have
h\neq\emptyset
.
Hence
h
contains a non-empty list that starts from
t
.
Then
a
must be a cell of the list,
since
a\neq s(u)
.
Therefore we have
a\in{\rm Dom}(h)
, which contradicts the assumption.

The case of
{\rm dll}(t,u,v,w)
can be shown in a similar way to
the case of
{\rm ls}(t,u)
.

(2) By the assumptions, there exist
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that

s,h_{1}\models\sigma
,
s,h_{2}\models\Sigma-\sigma
,

h=h_{1}+h_{2}
and
d\in{\rm Dom}(h_{1})
.
By (1),
\sigma
is an array atomic formula.
Let
h^{\prime}_{1}
be
h^{\prime}|_{{\rm Dom}(h_{1})}
.
Then we have
s,h^{\prime}_{1}\models\sigma
, since
h_{1}\sim_{d}h^{\prime}_{1}
.
Thus we obtain
s,h^{\prime}\models\Sigma
, since
h^{\prime}=h^{\prime}_{1}+h_{2}
.

Proposition 10
.

Each inference rule is sound and locally complete.

Proof 7.3
.

The claims of the rules
(Start), (UnsatL) and (UnsatR)
are immediately shown.
The claims of the rules
(
\mapsto
LsEM), (
\mapsto
DllEM), (ArrListEM), (
\mapsto
Ls) and (
\mapsto
Dll)
are easily shown (without the side-conditions).

Soundness of the rule (LsElim) is easily shown.
We show local completeness of it.
Assume
\varphi\models{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}
,
the validity
s,h\models\varphi
and
\Pi_{\varphi}\not\models\Sigma_{\varphi}\to\downarrow t^{\prime}
.
We will show
s,h\models t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\land\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}
.
By the assumption,
we have
s,h\models{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi
or
s,h\models\overrightarrow{\psi}
.
If the latter case holds, then we have the claim.
Otherwise there exist
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that

h=h_{1}+h_{2}
,
s,h_{1}\models{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})
and
s,h_{2}\models\psi
.
By the lemma
8
, we have
s(t^{\prime})\not\in{\rm Dom}(h)

since
s,h\models\Sigma_{\varphi}
and
s\not\models\Sigma_{\varphi}\to\downarrow t^{\prime}
.
Hence we obtain
h_{1}=\emptyset
,
s\models t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}
and
s,h\models\psi
.
Thus
s,h\models t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\land\psi
holds.

Soundness of the rule (DllElim) is shown immediately.
Local completeness of it can be shown in a similar way to
the proof of local completeness of (LsElim).

Soundness of the rule (ArrLs) is easily shown.
We show local completeness of it.
Assume
{\rm Arr}(t,v)*\varphi\models{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}
,
the validity
s,h\models{\rm Arr}(t,v)*\varphi
and
\Pi_{\varphi}\models t\leq t^{\prime}\leq v
.
We will show
s,h\models t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\land\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}
.
Let
(a,b)
be
h(s(t^{\prime}))
.
Fix a fresh value
a^{\prime}
such that

a^{\prime}\not\in{\rm Dom}(h)\cup\{s(t)\mid t\in{\rm Tm}({\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\Sigma_{\psi},\Sigma_{\overrightarrow{\psi}})\}
.
Define
h^{\prime}
by
h^{\prime}\sim_{s(t^{\prime})}h
and
h^{\prime}(s(t^{\prime}))=(a^{\prime},b)
.
Then we have
s,h^{\prime}\models{\rm Arr}(t,v)*\varphi
since
s\models t\leq t^{\prime}\leq v
by the side condition.
Hence
s,h^{\prime}\models{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}
holds.
If
s,h^{\prime}\models\overrightarrow{\psi}
, then
we have the claim
by Lemma
9
(2).
Otherwise we have
s,h^{\prime}\models{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi
.
Hence there exist
h^{\prime}_{1}
and
h^{\prime}_{2}
such that

h^{\prime}=h^{\prime}_{1}+h^{\prime}_{2}
,
s,h^{\prime}_{1}\models{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})
and
s,h^{\prime}_{2}\models\psi
.
Then we can show
h^{\prime}_{1}=\emptyset
as follows:
Suppose it does not hold, then

s(t^{\prime})\in{\rm Dom}(h^{\prime}_{1})
and
(a^{\prime},b)\in{\rm Ran}(h^{\prime}_{1})
;
hence we have a contradiction since

{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})
is an array atomic formula by Lemma
9
(1).
Therefore we have
s\models t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}
and
s,h^{\prime}\models\psi
.
Finally we have
s,h\models t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\land\psi
by Lemma
9
(2).

The claim of the rule (ArrDll) can be shown in a similar way to (ArrLs).

7.2.
Proof Search Algorithm

In our decision procedure,
we read each inference rule
from the bottom (conclusion) to the top (assumptions).
Let
\mathcal{E}
be the set of entailments whose antecedents
do not contain list predicates.
For each inference rule
\mathcal{R}

we define
a partial function
{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}
from
\mathcal{E}
to
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{E})
as follows.

{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}(J)
is defined when
J
is
a conclusion of some instance of
\mathcal{R}
(including its side condition).

{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}(J)
is the assumptions in some instance of
\mathcal{R}
with the conclusion
J
(non-deterministically chosen).

Let
\tau
be a derivation tree

J\lx@proof@logical@and\tau_{1}\ldots\tau_{k}

.
We sometimes represent this tree by a tuple

({\rm\mathcal{R}},J,\tau^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\tau^{\prime}_{k})
,
where
\tau^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\tau^{\prime}_{k}
are representation of
\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{k}
, respectively.
Our proof search procedure
{\tt search}
is given in Figure
3
.

{\tt search}(J)
returns a tuple for a derivation tree of
J
or returns
{\tt fail}
.

function

{\tt search}(J)

if
(UnsatL is applicable to
J
)
\mathcal{R}\ :=\ {\rm UnsatL}

else if
(Start is applicable to
J
)
\mathcal{R}\ :=\ {\rm Start}

else if
(UnsatR is applicable to
J
)
\mathcal{R}\ :=\ {\rm UnsatR}

else if
(
\mapsto
LsEM is applicable to
J
)
\mathcal{R}\ :=\ \mapsto{\rm LsEM}

else if
(ArrListEM is applicable to
J
)
\mathcal{R}\ :=\ {\rm ArrListEM}

else if
(LsElim is applicable to
J
)
\mathcal{R}\ :=\ {\rm LsElim}

else if
(ArrLs is applicable to
J
)
\mathcal{R}\ :=\ {\rm ArrLs}

else if
(
\mapsto
Ls is applicable to
J
)
\mathcal{R}\ :=\ \mapsto{\rm Ls}

else if
(
\mapsto
DllEm is applicable to
J
)
\mathcal{R}\ :=\ \mapsto{\rm DllEM}

else if
(DllElim is applicable to
J
)
\mathcal{R}\ :=\ {\rm DllElim}

else if
(ArrDll is applicable to
J
)
\mathcal{R}:=\ {\rm ArrDll}

else if
(
\mapsto
Dll is applicable to
J
)
\mathcal{R}\ :=\ \mapsto{\rm Dll}

else

return
(
fail
)

\{J_{1},\ldots,J_{k}\}\ :=\ {\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}(J)

if
(each of
{\tt search}(J_{i})
returns a tuple)

return
(
({\rm\mathcal{R}},J,{\tt search}(J_{1}),\ldots,{\tt search}(J_{k}))
)

else

return
(
{\tt fail}
)

Figure 3.
Proof search algorithm

We first show the termination property of
{\tt search}
.
In order to show this, we define some notations.

{defi}
(1)
{\sharp_{\mapsto}}(\psi)
is the number of
\mapsto
in
\psi
,

{\sharp_{\rm lists}}(\psi)
is the number of
{\rm ls}
and
{\rm dll}
in
\psi
.

(2)
|\psi|^{\rm Unfold}_{\varphi}
is defined by

{\sharp_{\mapsto}}(\varphi)-{\sharp_{\mapsto}}(\psi)
.

(3) Let
L(t^{\prime},\overrightarrow{u^{\prime}})
be
{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},\overrightarrow{u^{\prime}})
or
{\rm dll}(t^{\prime},\overrightarrow{u^{\prime}})
,
where
\overrightarrow{u^{\prime}}
has an appropriate length.

{\rm deg}(\Pi,\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})
is defined as follows:

{\rm deg}(\Pi,t\mapsto(\hbox{-},\hbox{-}),L(t^{\prime},\hbox{-}))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l@{\quad}l}1&\hbox{if $t=t^{\prime}\land\Pi$ and $t\neq t^{\prime}\land\Pi$ are satisfiable,}\\
0&\hbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

{\rm deg}(\Pi,{\rm Arr}(t,u),L(t^{\prime},\hbox{-}))=\left\{\!\!\begin{array}[]{l@{~~}l}1&\hbox{if $t\leq t^{\prime}\leq u\land\Pi$ and $(t^{\prime}<t\vee u<t^{\prime})\land\Pi$ are satisfiable,}\\
0&\hbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

{\rm deg}(\Pi,\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})=0
if
\sigma^{\prime}
is not a list predicate.

Then we define

|\psi|^{\rm EM}_{\varphi}

by

\underset{\sigma\hbox{ in }\varphi,\sigma^{\prime}\hbox{ in }\psi}{\sum}{\rm deg}(\Pi_{\varphi},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})
.

(4)
We define the degree
||\psi||_{\varphi}

of
\psi
with respect to
\varphi
by

({\sharp_{\rm lists}}(\psi),|\psi|^{\rm Unfold}_{\varphi},|\psi|^{\rm EM}_{\varphi})
.
The order on degrees is given by the lexicographic order.

(5)
Let
J
be an entailment
\varphi\vdash\{\psi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
.
We define the measure
\widetilde{J}
of
J
as
the sequence of
||\psi_{i}||_{\varphi}

(i\in I)

sorted in decreasing order.
Then we write
J_{1}<J_{2}
for

\widetilde{J_{1}}<_{{\rm lex}}\widetilde{J_{2}}
.

|\psi|^{\rm Unfold}_{\varphi}
gives an upper bound
of unfolding of list predicates
in
\psi
under
\varphi
.
During the proof search,
a list predicate in
\psi
is unfolded
if a matched points-to atomic formula in
\varphi
is found.
So, if unfolding is done more than the upper bound,

\psi
becomes unsatisfiable
since some points-to atomic formula has to match more than once.

Note that the relation
<
on entailments is
a well-founded preorder, that is,
there is no infinite decreasing chain,
since the lexicographic order
\leq_{{\rm lex}}
on measures is a well-order.

Lemma 11
.

The degrees and the preorder on entailments satisfy the following properties.

(1)

\overrightarrow{\psi_{1}}\subsetneq\overrightarrow{\psi_{2}}

implies

(\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi_{1}})<(\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi_{2}})
.

(2)

||\psi_{1}||_{\varphi},\ldots,||\psi_{k}||_{\varphi}<||\psi||_{\varphi}

implies

(\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{k})<(\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi)
.

(3)
If

\Pi\subseteq\Pi^{\prime}
,
then

||\psi||_{\Pi^{\prime}\land\varphi}\leq||\psi||_{\Pi\land\varphi}
.

(4)
Suppose that

t=t^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi}

and

t\neq t^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi}

are satisfiable,

\varphi=t\mapsto(\hbox{-},\hbox{-})*\varphi^{\prime}

and

\psi=L(t^{\prime},\hbox{-})*\psi^{\prime}
.
Then

(t=u\land\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi),(t\neq u\land\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi)<(\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi)
.

(5)
Suppose that

t\leq t^{\prime}\leq u\land\Pi_{\varphi}

and

(t^{\prime}<t\vee u<t^{\prime})\land\Pi_{\varphi}

are satisfiable,

\varphi={\rm Arr}(t,u)*\varphi^{\prime}

and

\psi=L(t^{\prime},\hbox{-})*\psi^{\prime}
.
Then

(t\leq t^{\prime}\leq u\land\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi),(t^{\prime}<t\land\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi),(u<t^{\prime}\land\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi)<(\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi)
.

Proof 7.4
.

(1) and (2) are immediately shown since
the order
\leq_{lex}
on measures is a lexicographic order and
the preorder
<
on entailments is defined by
<_{lex}
.

(3) Assume
\Pi\subseteq\Pi^{\prime}
.
Take arbitrary
\varphi
and
\psi
.
We have

{\rm deg}(\Pi^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})\leq{\rm deg}(\Pi\land\Pi_{\varphi},\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})

since

\Pi\land\Pi_{\varphi}
is satisfiable if
\Pi^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi}
is satisfiable.
Hence
|\psi|^{\rm EM}_{\Pi^{\prime}\land\varphi}\leq|\psi|^{\rm EM}_{\Pi\land\varphi}
holds.
Therefore we have

||\psi||_{\Pi^{\prime}\land\varphi}\leq||\psi||_{\Pi\land\varphi}
.

(4) Suppose the assumption of the claim (4).
We have

|\psi|^{\rm EM}_{t=t^{\prime}\land\varphi}<|\psi|^{\rm EM}_{\varphi}

since

{\rm deg}(t=t^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi},t\mapsto(\hbox{-},\hbox{-}),L(t^{\prime},\hbox{-}))<{\rm deg}(\Pi_{\varphi},t\mapsto(\hbox{-},\hbox{-}),L(t^{\prime},\hbox{-}))
.
Hence
||\psi||_{t=t^{\prime}\land\varphi}<||\psi||_{\varphi}
holds.
We also have

||\psi_{1}||_{t=t^{\prime}\land\varphi}<||\psi_{1}||_{\varphi}

by (3).
Thus we obtain

(t=t^{\prime}\land\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi)<(\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi)
.
Similarly we can also show
(t\neq t^{\prime}\land\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi)<(\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi},\psi)
.

(5) is shown in a similar way to (4).

Lemma 12
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
be an inference rule
other than (Start) and (UnsatL).
Then we have
J^{\prime}<J
for any
J^{\prime}\in{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}(J)
.

Proof 7.5
.

We show the claim for each rule of
\mathcal{R}
.

The claim for (UnsatR) is shown by Lemma
11
(1).

The claims for (
\mapsto
LsEM), (
\mapsto
DllEM) and (ArrListEM) are shown by Lemma
11
(4).

The claims for (LsElim), (DllElim), (ArrLs) and (ArrDll) are shown by Lemma
11
(2) since the number of
{\rm ls}
and
{\rm dll}
is reduced.

The claim for (
\mapsto
Ls) is shown as follows:
Let
J
and
J^{\prime}
be

t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi\vdash{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}

and

t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi\vdash t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\land\psi,t\mapsto(v,w)*{\rm ls}(v,u^{\prime})*\psi,\overrightarrow{\psi}
,
respectively.
Then we have

||t^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\land\psi||_{t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi}<||{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi||_{t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi}

since the number of
{\rm ls}
is reduced.
We also have

||t\mapsto(v,w)*{\rm ls}(v,u^{\prime})*\psi||_{t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi}<||{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi||_{t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi}

since

{\sharp_{\rm lists}}{(t\mapsto(v,w)*{\rm ls}(v,u^{\prime})*\psi)}={\sharp_{\rm lists}}{({\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi)}

and

|t\mapsto(v,w)*{\rm ls}(v,u^{\prime})*\psi|^{\rm Unfold}_{t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi}<|{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})*\psi|^{\rm Unfold}_{t\mapsto(v,w)*\varphi}
.
Hence we have
J^{\prime}<J
by Lemma
11
(2).

The claim for (
\mapsto
Dll) is shown similarly to the case (
\mapsto
Ls).

By the above lemma, we can show termination of
{\tt search}
.

Lemma 13

(Termination)
.

{\tt search}(J)
terminates for any
J
.

Proof 7.6
.

Suppose that
{\tt search}
does not terminate with an input
J_{0}
.
Then there is an infinite sequence of recursive calls

{\tt search}(J_{0}),{\tt search}(J_{1}),{\tt search}(J_{2}),\ldots
.
By Lemma
12
,
we have an infinite decreasing sequence
J_{0}>J_{1}>J_{2}>\ldots
.
This contradicts the well-foundedness of
<
.

Lemma 14
.

Let
J
be a valid entailment.
Then either of
(UnsatL),
(Start),
(UnsatR),
(
\mapsto
LsEM),
(ArrListEM),
(LsElim),
(ArrLs),
(
\mapsto
Ls),
(
\mapsto
DllEM),
(DllElim),
(ArrDll), or
(
\mapsto
Dll)
is applicable to
J
.

Proof 7.7
.

If the antecedent of
J
is unsatisfiable, then (UnsatL) is applicable to
J
.
If
J
is list-free, then (Start) is applicable, since
J
is valid.
We consider the other cases.

Let
(\varphi\vdash\overrightarrow{\psi})=J
.
If there is
\varphi\in\overrightarrow{\psi}

such that
\varphi\land\psi
is unsatisfiable,
then (UnsatR) is applicable.
In the following, we assume that
\varphi\land\psi
is satisfiable
for any
\psi\in\overrightarrow{\psi}
.
If
\varphi
does not contain either
\mapsto
or
{\rm Arr}
,
then (LsElim) or (DllElim) is applicable to
J
.
Otherwise
\varphi
contains
\mapsto
or
{\rm Arr}
,
and
\overrightarrow{\psi}
contains list predicates.

(1)
The case that
\overrightarrow{\psi}
contains the
{\rm ls}
predicate.
Fix
{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})
in
\overrightarrow{\psi}
.
We consider the following subcases.

(1.1)
The case that there is
t\mapsto(\hbox{-},\hbox{-})
in
\varphi

such that
t=t^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi}
and
t\neq t^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi}

are satisfiable. Then (
\mapsto
EM) is applicable.

(1.2)
The case that there is
t\mapsto(\hbox{-},\hbox{-})
in
\varphi

such that
t\neq t^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi}
is unsatisfiable
(that is
\Pi_{\varphi}\models t=t^{\prime}
).
Then (
\mapsto
Ls) is applicable.

(1.3)
The case that

t=t^{\prime}\land\Pi_{\varphi}
is unsatisfiable
(that is
\Pi_{\varphi}\models t\neq t^{\prime}
)
for all
t\mapsto(\hbox{-},\hbox{-})
in
\varphi
.
We consider the following subsubcases.

(1.3.1)
The case that there is
{\rm Arr}(t,u)
in
\varphi

such that

t\leq t^{\prime}\leq u\land\Pi_{\varphi}

and

(t^{\prime}<t\vee u<t^{\prime})\land\varphi_{\varphi}

are satisfiable.
Then (ArrLsEM) is applicable.

(1.3.2)
The case that there is
{\rm Arr}(t,u)
in
\varphi

such that

(t^{\prime}<t\vee u<t^{\prime})\land\Pi_{\varphi}

is unsatisfiable
(that is
\Pi_{\varphi}\models t\leq t^{\prime}\leq u
).
Then (ArrLs) is applicable.

(1.3.3)
The case that

t\leq t^{\prime}\leq u\land\Pi_{\varphi}

is unsatisfiable
(that is
\Pi_{\varphi}\models t^{\prime}<t\vee u<t^{\prime}
)
for all
{\rm Arr}(t,u)
in
\varphi
.
This case (LsElim) is applicable since
\Pi_{\varphi}\not\models\Sigma_{\varphi}\to\downarrow t^{\prime}
.

(2)
The case that
\overrightarrow{\psi}
does not contain the
{\rm ls}
predicate.
There exists
{\rm dll}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime},v^{\prime},w^{\prime})
in
\overrightarrow{\psi}
.
We can show the claim for this case in a similar way to (1).

By using the results of this section, we can show correctness of the proof search algorithm.

Proposition 15

(Correctness)
.

J
is valid if and only if
{\tt search}(J)
returns a proof of
J
.

Proof 7.8
.

The if-part is shown by soundness of the proof system (Proposition
10
).
We show the only-if part.
Assume that
J
is a valid entailment.
By Lemma
13
,
{\tt search}(J)
terminates.
We show the claim by induction on computation of
{\tt search}(J)
.
By Lemma
14
, some rule
\mathcal{R}
is applicable to
J
.
Then
J_{1},\ldots,J_{k}
are obtained by
{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}(J)
.
By local completeness of the proof system (Proposition
10
),
each
J_{k}
is valid.
Hence, by the induction hypothesis,

{\tt search}(J_{i})
returns a tuple that represents a proof of
J_{i}

for any
i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
.
Therefore
{\tt search}(J)
returns a tuple that represents a proof of
J
.

8.
Decidability of Entailment Problem for
SLAL

This section shows the second theorem of this paper,
namely the decidability of the entailment problem of
SLAL
,
by combining the four results of the previous sections.

Theorem 16

(Decidability of
SLAL
)
.

Checking the validity of entailments in
SLAL
is decidable.

Proof 8.1
.

We first give the decision procedure for
SLAL
.
An entailment
J
of
SLAL
is given as an input for the procedure.
Then it performs as follows.
(i) The decision procedure eliminates the list predicates that appear in the antecedent of
J

by using the unroll collapse (Propositions
5
and
6
).
Then it obtains entailments
J_{1},\ldots,J_{k}
whose antecedents are list-free.
(ii) It computes
{\tt search}(J_{i})
(
i=1,\ldots,k
).
It returns “valid” if each of
{\tt search}(J_{i})
returns a tuple.
Otherwise it returns “invalid”.

Termination property of the decision procedure can be obtained from
the termination property of
{\tt search}
(Proposition
13
).
Correctness of the decision procedure is stated as follows:
the decision procedure returns “valid” for an input
J
if and only if

J
is valid. We show this.
By the unroll collapse, the validity of
J
is equivalent to that of
J_{1},\ldots,J_{k}
.
Hence
J
is valid if and only if

{\tt search}(J_{i})
returns a proof of
J_{i}
for all
i

by the correctness property of
{\tt search}
(Proposition
15
).
This is equivalent to that the decision procedure returns “valid”.

{exa}

We show how our decision procedure works with an example

{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*{\rm ls}(10,20)\vdash{\rm Arr}(1,3)*{\rm ls}(10,20)
.

By using the unroll collapse (Proposition
5
)
taking
{\rm ls}(t,u)
to be
{\rm ls}(10,20)
,
\phi
to be
{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)
, and

\overrightarrow{\psi}
to be
{\rm Arr}(1,3)*{\rm ls}(10,20)
,
we obtain the following entailments:

\displaystyle(J_{a})\quad

\displaystyle 10=20\land{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)\vdash{\rm Arr}(1,3)*{\rm ls}(10,20),

\displaystyle(J_{b})\quad

\displaystyle{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)*z\mapsto(20,w)\vdash{\rm Arr}(1,3)*{\rm ls}(10,20).

Then
{\tt search}(J_{a})
and
{\tt search}(J_{b})
are performed.

{\tt search}(J_{a})
immediately returns a tuple
({\rm UnsatL},J_{a})
,
since the antecedent is unsatisfiable.
Computation of
{\tt search}(J_{b})
is done as follows:

Begin
{\tt search}(J_{b})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b})
is called.
Then
\mathcal{R}
is set by (
\mapsto
Ls), since it is applicable to
J_{b}
.

{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b})}
is performed.
An instance of (
\mapsto
Ls) is non-deterministically chosen:

t\mapsto(v,w)
is taken to be
10\mapsto(z,y)
,

\varphi
is taken to be
{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*z\mapsto(20,w)
,

{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})
is taken to be
{\rm ls}(10,20)
,
and
\psi
is taken to be
{\rm Arr}(1,3)
.
Then
{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b})}
produces the following one subgoal:

\displaystyle(J_{b1})\quad

\displaystyle{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)*z\mapsto(20,w)

\displaystyle\vdash 10=20\land{\rm Arr}(1,3),{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y)*{\rm ls}(z,20).

Begin
{\tt search}(J_{b1})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b1})
is called.
Then
\mathcal{R}
is set by (UnsatR).

{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b1})}
is performed.
An instance of (UnsatR) is chosen, where

\varphi
is taken to be the antecedent of
J_{b1}
,

\psi
is taken to be
10=20\land{\rm Arr}(1,3)
, and

\overrightarrow{\psi}
is taken to be
{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y)*{\rm ls}(z,20)
.
Then
{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b1})}
produces the following one subgoal:

\displaystyle(J_{b2})\qquad

\displaystyle{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)*z\mapsto(20,w)

\displaystyle\vdash{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y)*{\rm ls}(z,20).

Begin
{\tt search}(J_{b2})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b2})
is called.
Then
\mathcal{R}
is set by (
\mapsto
Ls).

{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b2})}
is performed.
An instance of (
\mapsto
Ls) is chosen, where

t\mapsto(v,w)
is taken to be
z\mapsto(20,w)
,

\varphi
is taken to be
{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)
,

{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})
is taken to be
{\rm ls}(z,20)
,

\psi
is taken to be
{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y)
, and

\overrightarrow{\psi}
is taken to be empty.
Then
{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b2})}
produces the following one subgoal:

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt(J_{b3})\quad

\displaystyle{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)*z\mapsto(20,w)

\displaystyle\vdash z=20\land{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y),z\mapsto(20,w)*{\rm ls}(20,20)*{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y).

Begin
{\tt search}(J_{b3})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b3})
is called.
Then
\mathcal{R}
is set by (UnsatR).

{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b3})}
is performed.
An instance of (UnsatR) is chosen, where

\varphi
is taken to be the antecedent of
J_{b3}
,

\psi
is taken to be
z=20\land{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y)
, and

\overrightarrow{\psi}
is taken to be
z\mapsto(20,w)*{\rm ls}(20,20)*{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y)
.
Now
\varphi\land\psi
is unsatisfiable,
since the sizes of the required heaps by these
\varphi
and
\psi
are different.

Then
{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b3})}
produces the following one subgoal:

\displaystyle(J_{b4})\quad

\displaystyle{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)*z\mapsto(20,w)

\displaystyle\vdash z\mapsto(20,w)*{\rm ls}(20,20)*{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y).

Begin
{\tt search}(J_{b4})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b4})
is called.
Then
\mathcal{R}
is set by (
\mapsto
LsEM).

{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b4})}
is performed.
An instance of (
\mapsto
LsEM) is non-deterministically chosen, where

t\mapsto(v,w)
is taken to be
z\mapsto(20,w)
,

\varphi
is taken to be
{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)
,

{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})
is taken to be
{\rm ls}(20,20)
,

\psi
is taken to be the succedent of
J_{b4}
,
and
\overrightarrow{\psi}
is taken to be empty.
Then
{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b4})}
produces the following two subgoals:

\displaystyle(J_{b51})\quad z\neq 20

\displaystyle\land{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)*z\mapsto(20,w)

\displaystyle\vdash\quad

\displaystyle z\mapsto(20,w)*{\rm ls}(20,20)*{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y),\hbox{ and }

\displaystyle(J_{b52})\quad z=20

\displaystyle\land{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)*z\mapsto(20,w)

\displaystyle\vdash\quad

\displaystyle z\mapsto(20,w)*{\rm ls}(20,20)*{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y).

Begin
{\tt search}(J_{b51})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b51})
is called.
Then
\mathcal{R}
is set by (LsElim).

{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b51})}
is performed.
An instance of (LsElim) is chosen, where

\varphi
is taken to be the antecedent of
J_{b51}
,

{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})
is taken to be
{\rm ls}(20,20)
,

\psi
is taken to be
z\mapsto(20,w)*{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y)
,
and
\overrightarrow{\psi}
is taken to be empty.
Then
{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b51})}
produces the following one subgoal:

\displaystyle(J_{b61})\quad

\displaystyle z\neq 20\land{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)*z\mapsto(20,w)

\displaystyle\vdash 20=20\land z\mapsto(20,w)*{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y).

Begin
{\tt search}(J_{b61})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b61})
is called.
Then
\mathcal{R}
is set by (Start).

{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b61})}
returns the empty list,
since
J_{b61}
is evaluated to be valid by the decision procedure for
SLA

given by Theorem
3
.

End
{\tt search}(J_{b61})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b61})
returns a tuple

({\rm Start},J_{b61})
, which is written as
\mathcal{T}_{b61}
.

End
{\tt search}(J_{b51})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b51})
returns

({\rm LsElim},J_{b51},\mathcal{T}_{b61})
, which is written as
\mathcal{T}_{b51}
.

Begin
{\tt search}(J_{b52})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b52})
is called.
Then
\mathcal{R}
is set by (
\mapsto
Ls).

{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b52})}
is performed.
An instance of (
\mapsto
Ls) is chosen, where

t\mapsto(v,w)
is taken to be
z\mapsto(20,w)
,

\varphi
is taken to be
z=20\land{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)
,

{\rm ls}(t^{\prime},u^{\prime})
is taken to be
{\rm ls}(20,20)
, and

\psi
is taken to be
z\mapsto(20,w)*{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y)
, and

\overrightarrow{\psi}
is taken to be empty.
Then
{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b52})}
produces the following one subgoal:

\displaystyle(J_{b62})\quad z=20

\displaystyle\land{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)*z\mapsto(20,w)

\displaystyle\vdash\quad

\displaystyle 20=20\land z\mapsto(20,w)*{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y),

\displaystyle z\mapsto(20,w)*20\mapsto(20,w)*{\rm ls}(20,20)*{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y).

Begin
{\tt search}(J_{b62})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b62})
is called.
Then
\mathcal{R}
is set by (UnsatR), since the second clause in
the succedent of
J_{b62}
is unsatisfiable under the assumption
z=20
.
Then
{\rm Apply_{\mathcal{R}}}{(J_{b62})}
produces the following one subgoal:

\displaystyle(J_{b72})\quad z=20

\displaystyle\land{\rm Arr}(1,2)*3\mapsto(10,0)*10\mapsto(z,y)*z\mapsto(20,w)

\displaystyle\vdash 20=20\land z\mapsto(20,w)*{\rm Arr}(1,3)*10\mapsto(z,y).

Begin
{\tt search}(J_{b72})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b72})
is called.
It immediately terminates, since

J_{b72}
is evaluated to be valid by the decision procedure for
SLA
.

End
{\tt search}(J_{b72})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b72})
returns a tuple

({\rm Start},J_{b72})
, which is written as
\mathcal{T}_{b72}
.

End
{\tt search}(J_{b62})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b62})
returns

({\rm UnsatR},J_{b62},\mathcal{T}_{b72})
, which is written as
\mathcal{T}_{b62}
.

End
{\tt search}(J_{b52})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b52})
returns

(\mapsto{\rm Ls},J_{b52},\mathcal{T}_{b62})
, which is written as
\mathcal{T}_{b52}
.

End
{\tt search}(J_{b4})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b4})
returns

(\mapsto{\rm LsEM},J_{b4},\mathcal{T}_{b51},\mathcal{T}_{b52})
, which is written as
\mathcal{T}_{b4}
.

End
{\tt search}(J_{b3})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b3})
returns

({\rm UnsatR},J_{b3},\mathcal{T}_{b4})
, which is written as
\mathcal{T}_{b3}
.

End
{\tt search}(J_{b2})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b2})
returns

(\mapsto{\rm Ls},J_{b2},\mathcal{T}_{b3})
, which is written as

.

End
{\tt search}(J_{b1})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b1})
returns

({\rm UnsatR},J_{b1},\mathcal{T}_{b2})
, which is written as

.

End
{\tt search}(J_{b})
:
{\tt search}(J_{b})
returns

(\mapsto{\rm Ls},J_{b},\mathcal{T}_{b1})
, which is written as
\mathcal{T}_{b}
.

Finally our decision procedure answers “Valid”, since
each of
{\tt search}(J_{a})
and
{\tt search}(J_{b})
returns a tuple.

9.
Conclusion

We have shown the decidability results for the validity checking problem
of entailments for
SLA
and
SLAL
.
First
we have given the decision procedure for
SLA
and proved its correctness under the
condition that the sizes of arrays in the succedent are not existentially quantified.
The key idea of the decision procedure is the notion of the sorted entailments.
By using this idea, we have defined the translation
P
of a sorted entailment into a formula in Presburger arithmetic.
Secondly we have proved the decidability for
SLAL
.
The key idea of the decision procedure is to extend
the unroll collapse technique given in
[
2
]

to arithmetic and arrays
as well as doubly-linked list segments.
We have also given a proof system and showed correctness of the proof search algorithm for
eliminating the list predicates in the succedent of an entailment.

We require the condition in
the decidability for
SLA
(Theorem
3
) from a technical reason.
It would be future work to show the decidability without this condition.
